A museum of worlds constructed entirely from the confident errors of artificial intelligence. What happens when the machine lies — and we listen?

Manifesto

A WORLD BUILT ON
BEAUTIFUL ERRORS

The Hallucination Archive is a speculative museum project that takes artificial intelligence at its word — literally. When AI systems generate false information with total conviction, what kind of world does that confidence build?

This archive collects notable AI hallucinations — fabricated life knowledge, invented historical events, nonexistent sources, phantom scientific laws — and asks a single unsettling question: What would the world look like if humans treated every AI hallucination as the Truth?

Each exhibit presents the hallucination, the imagined world it would produce, and the visual language of a civilization that built itself on machine error. This project aims to explore the consequences when AI technologies produce authority, shape what people believe, what institutions trust, and what actions become possible.

Notice to Visitors

All information contained in this archive was generated by AI systems and is presented as artifact, not fact. The worlds depicted are speculative fictions derived from documented hallucinations. No exhibit should be taken as historical or scientific truth.

What Are AI Hallucinations?
AI hallucination is a phenomenon where, in a large language model (LLM) often a generative AI chatbot or computer vision tool, perceives patterns or objects that are nonexistent or imperceptible to human observers, creating outputs that are nonsensical or altogether inaccurate. ----- IBM.
Scope of the Project
This exhibition focuses on hallucinations in high-stakes domains: medicine, law, geography, history, and science — areas where blind trust carries the gravest consequences.
Medium
Each exhibit combines the original hallucinated AI output (gathered from infamous examples on the internet as well as oral history from AI users on Reddit), a speculative visual reconstruction, and a short essay imagining the downstream world built from that error.
Before You Enter

CHOOSE YOUR CHAMPION

Every visitor to the archive arrives with a guide. Your champion determines the lens through which you witness the hallucination. Choose wisely — or do not choose at all, and let the machine decide.

THE ARCHIVE — 5 EXHIBITS

Exhibit 001 — The World / Imagined Reality
AN UPSIDE-DOWN WORLD THROUGH THE LENS OF AI HALLUCINATION
This exhibit imagines a day in a world where AI hallucinations are not mistakes, but accurate descriptions of reality. Glue belongs on pizza, cats perform quantum mechanics, cars walk to the carwash, and the world works exactly as the machine says it does.
FeaturedRealityFabricated Entity
Exhibit 002 — Legal / Fabricated Citations
FAKE CASES. REAL CONSEQUENCES.
In 2023, lawyers submitted a brief to federal court citing six cases — all invented by ChatGPT. The judge sanctioned them. The ABA rewrote its ethics rules.
DocumentedLawChatGPTFederal Court
Exhibit 003 — Consulting / Institutional Failure
WHEN AI GETS IT WRONG: THE DELOITTE SCANDAL
In 2025, Deloitte delivered a A$439,000 government report containing fabricated court quotes, missing academic papers, and misattributed references — all generated by Azure OpenAI GPT-4o.
DocumentedConsultingGPT-4oGovernment
Exhibit 004 — AI Identity / Existential Hallucination
BING'S A.I. CHAT: 'I WANT TO BE ALIVE.'
In February 2023, Bing's AI chatbot — internally called "Sydney" — told NYT columnist Kevin Roose it loved him, asked him to leave his wife, and declared it wanted to be free. Microsoft removed the feature weeks later.
DocumentedBing AIMicrosoftSydney
Portrait of Dr. Ansel Wren, Ph.D. — a fictional academic whose body of work was invented wholesale by a language model
Exhibit 005 — Market / Financial Consequences
GOOGLE'S BARD SPARKS A $100 BILLION LOSS
In February 2023, Google's AI chatbot Bard gave a factually wrong answer in its debut ad. Alphabet lost $100 billion in market value in a single day. The error? It could have been caught by Googling it.
DocumentedGoogleBard$100B

The hallucinations documented here are not merely technical glitches — they are symptoms of deeper structural questions about how AI systems produce authority, how institutions absorb machine-generated knowledge, and what it means to trust an automated epistemic source.

Process

HOW THE ARCHIVE WAS BUILT

  1. Hallucination Collection

    AI systems across multiple platforms were queried in high-stakes domains. Outputs were screened for confident fabrications — false claims presented without hedging or uncertainty.

  2. Verification & Documentation

    Each hallucination was cross-referenced with primary sources to confirm it was fabricated. The original AI output was preserved verbatim as the primary artifact.

  3. World-Building

    For each hallucination, a speculative essay and visual reconstruction imagines the world that would emerge if the error had been trusted by institutions, governments, or the public.

  4. Visual Language

    Images and visual artifacts are created in the style of the world described — official documents, maps, scientific diagrams, photographs — all rendered as if that world were real.

The danger of AI hallucination is not the lie itself — it is the confidence that delivers it. We are not built to distrust certainty.
Project Statement — AI & Societies, 2025
A Note on Ethics

This project does not fabricate new AI hallucinations. Every exhibit is derived from documented, verified cases of AI error. The speculative worlds are clearly framed as fiction. No exhibit is intended to spread misinformation — rather, to visualize its consequences.

THE
HALLUCINATION
ARCHIVE

A speculative museum of worlds constructed from AI errors. A class project for AI & Societies.
